### 46. ★★★☆☆ Campaigners claim minister attempting to undermine 'Holiday Hunger Bill'

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: BBC Business
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T08:09:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: The education minister has warned it would immediately cost his department £20m a year at a time when it is already struggling to balance its budget.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Image caption, Paul Givan has now proposed amendments which would give him discretion to decide when the payments would take effect
Campaigners have accused the education minister of attempting to undermine a proposed Stormont law which would help poorer families with the cost of food.
The Democratic Unionist Party&#x27;s (DUP) Paul Givan has been consistently critical of the legislation which is being brought forward by Sinn FÃ©in assembly member Danny Baker.
Givan has warned it would immediately cost his department Â£20m a year at a time when it is already struggling to balance its budget.
The Holiday Meals Payment Bill , external would create a new payment for families whose children receive free school meals which would be made during school holidays to help with the cost of food.
The matter is due to be discussed in the assembly on Monday.
Givan has now proposed amendments which would give him the power to delay implementation.
He will need support from other parties for the amendments to be accepted.
It is not clear if he will get that support, although the Ulster Unionist Party (UUP) has expressed reservations about the proposal.
Image caption, The payment would be made during school holidays to help with the cost of food
As it is based on school meals, the cost of the scheme would be on the Department of Education.
It is modelled on the School Holiday Food Grant scheme introduced during the pandemic, which paid qualifying families Â£27 per child every fortnight.
In April, Givan warned MLAs that his department did not have funding for the scheme, so they would be voting to cut education spending if they supported it.
He has now proposed amendments which would give him discretion to decide when the payments would take effect.
A coalition of campaign groups, including the National Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children (NSPCC) and Save the Children, say the amendments would mean "no guarantees about when, if ever, payments might begin".
They added: "We are calling on all political parties and all MLAs to reject the amendments tabled by the education minister and support the bill as it currently stands."
Baker has said the education minister is attempting to "obstruct" the implementation of the bill.
"His proposals would give him control over when, if ever, holiday hunger payments begin.
"This is an attempt to delay and undermine legislation that has the support of the majority of parties in the assembly."
A Department of Education spokesperson said the minister&#x27;s amendments "do not seek to delay or block the Bill".
"They reflect the Department&#x27;s duty to manage its budget responsibly, taking account of financial pressures, competing priorities and the impact of new spending on existing services, particularly at a time when education faces significant financial challenges across the system," they added.
"The amendments fulfil that duty while preserving the Bill&#x27;s intended purpose. The Bill&#x27;s key provisions remain unchanged, including eligibility, payment levels, holiday coverage and the Education Authority&#x27;s responsibilities."
&#x27;You have to say no&#x27;: Families struggling with holiday food costs

### 47. ★★★☆☆ Not Nvidia. Not Broadcom. This Little-Known Chip Supplier Could Be the AI Boom's Best-Kept Secret. - The Motley Fool

- **分类**: 🤖 AI/科技
- **来源**: GN AI
- **多源确认**: 1
- **假设性事件**: 是（已降权）
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T00:30:00+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Not Nvidia. Not Broadcom. This Little-Known Chip Supplier Could Be the AI Boom's Best-Kept Secret.&nbsp;&nbsp;The Motley Fool

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

When investors think about the artificial intelligence boom, two names tend to dominate conversations. They are Nvidia ( NVDA -0.52% ) , which supplies the graphics processing units (GPUs) powering much of the AI industry, and Broadcom ( AVGO +0.39% ) , which helps tech companies develop custom silicon. But there&#x27;s a lesser-known player that could be the industry&#x27;s best-kept secret: Cerebras Systems ( CBRS -2.23% ) .
With Cerebras, it all starts with speed. Instead of the usual approach to chip design, Cerebras built its business around a large processor designed specifically for artificial intelligence : its Wafer-Scale Engine.
The chip combines computing cores, memory, and communication capabilities into a single piece of silicon, reducing the need to move data between separate components. Through this approach, Cerebras aims to alleviate bottlenecks that can slow AI workloads.
That matters because training an AI model is only one part of the equation. Once a model is deployed, it&#x27;s used for AI inference , answering questions, writing code, and even executing tasks for millions of users in real time. In fact, inference is becoming one of the fastest-growing trends in the AI market today.
Cerebras is investing in that shift by working to make AI responses faster.
As AI applications become more interactive, speed could become a huge competitive advantage. It could make conversations with AI chatbots faster and more fluid, while enabling AI agents to complete multi-step tasks more quickly, across domains ranging from software development and business workflows to scientific research.
The company&#x27;s approach is already attracting attention from major AI players, including OpenAI.
Getting a major AI company to adopt its technology is a huge vote of confidence for any chipmaker. That&#x27;s why Cerebras&#x27; relationship with OpenAI stands out.
The companies recently announced a multi-year agreement to deploy 750 megawatts of Cerebras compute capacity for OpenAI customers, with deployment planned in stages through 2028. The goal is to make AI responses faster, particularly for workloads that generate long answers, assist with coding, and power AI agents.
The partnership took an even bigger turn in August, when Cerebras announced that it&#x27;s powering the Ultrafast mode for OpenAI&#x27;s GPT-5.6 Sol. The company says the service can deliver up to 750 output tokens per second and run up to 14 times faster than with standard processing. This demonstrates how Cerebras&#x27; specialized hardware can greatly improve the user experience with advanced AI models.
For investors, the important point is that Cerebras&#x27; technology is already powering real commercial AI services, backed by one of the leading AI model providers. But proving its technology works is only part of the equation.
The company is also racing to build out its next-generation hardware.
Cerebras recently announced its CS-4 system, which it says can deliver up to twice the speed of its predecessor, along with improved efficiency and faster token generation. CS-4 is part of a broader platform that Cerebras says will allow it to upgrade computing, cooling, and power delivery more independently. That matters because performance doesn&#x27;t depend on chips alone.
Data centers must also deliver enough power, move data efficiently, and cool dense hardware. As a result, by adopting this design, the company is trying to improve the economics of running AI at scale. That makes Cerebras&#x27; technology better suited for large deployments, especially as AI data centers become bigger and more complex.
For investors looking beyond the biggest chipmakers, this continued product development is another reason to watch the company.
$39B Market cap calculated using publicly traded shares outstanding only. Does not include unlisted, private, or dual-class non-traded shares. Implied market cap may vary.
Nvidia and Broadcom may be providing much of the infrastructure behind AI, but the AI boom is also creating opportunities for lesser-known chipmakers. That could mean room for more than one winning chip design.
Looking ahead, Cerebras&#x27; focus on speed and inference could give it a major role in the next phase of the AI boom, especially as it launches broader platforms that can scale with AI data centers.
If demand for faster AI keeps growing, Cerebras could turn out to be one of the market&#x27;s strongest under-the-radar chip suppliers.

Why Are Nasdaq Futures Rising Premarket? MU, IREN, SLS, NIO, ASTS, WFF Stocks In Focus
The third-quarter earnings season begins this week, with JPMorgan Chase, Goldman Sachs, Citigroup, and Wells Fargo among the major banks reporting Tuesday, while ASML and TSMC put the AI trade to the test.
Oil prices eased after President Donald Trump announced a Russia-Ukraine energy ceasefire, though crude remained above $100 a barrel amid ongoing Middle East tensions.
Traders will also watch September CPI and PPI data, along with retail sales and Federal Reserve commentary this week, for clues on the rate trajectory.
U.S. stock futures were higher early Monday as investors geared up for the official start of the third-quarter earnings season, with major U.S. banks reporting and tech reports from ASML and TSMC this week.
As of 4:00 a.m. ET, Nasdaq, S&P 500, Dow and Russell 2000 futures gained 0.2%. The modest gains followed a strong week on Wall Street, with the S&P 500 and Nasdaq hitting record highs.
See what 10M+ investors are talking about. Get the Stocktwits Daily Rip for what retail is watching right now, free to your inbox
On Stocktwits, retail sentiment for the SPDR S&P 500 ETF (SPY), which tracks the S&P 500 Index, and Invesco QQQ Trust (QQQ), which tracks the Nasdaq-100 Index, has moved to 'bullish' from 'extremely bullish' last week.
Oil prices dipped after President Donald Trump declared an immediate "energy ceasefire" between Russia and Ukraine, though crude remained above $100 a barrel amid escalating Middle East tensions following a Houthi strike on Riyadh's airport over the weekend.
U.S. Treasury markets are closed on Monday for Columbus Day, even as U.S. stock markets remain open.
The Q3 reporting period begins this week with major financial institutions including JPMorgan Chase (JPM), Goldman Sachs (GS), Citigroup (C) and Wells Fargo (WFC) reporting on Tuesday, followed by Bank of America (BAC), Morgan Stanley (MS) and other major financial companies on Wednesday.
Meanwhile, September's Consumer Price Index is due on Wednesday, followed by Producer Price Index data and retail sales on Thursday. Fed Chair Kevin Warsh is scheduled to speak on Thursday at the 2026 Annual Meetings of the Boards of Governors of the International Monetary Fund (IMF) and World Bank Group.
Micron Technology (MU), Taiwan Semiconductor Manufacturing Co (TSM): Shares dipped 1% in early premarket trade as China's ChangXin Memory Technologies (CXMT) reportedly advanced work on next-generation DRAM technology. TSM will also report earnings later this week, with investors watching for commentary on AI demand.
Separately, Elon Musk's plans for Terafab, a proposed chipmaking initiative tied to Tesla (TSLA) and SpaceX (SPCX), have brought the semiconductor supply chain back into focus. Reports indicate TSMC is considering a role in the Texas project, and Intel is also advancing its involvement. Over the weekend, Musk unveiled plans for Terafab, aiming to iterate rapidly on 10 internal chip designs per year.
Apple (AAPL) is on the watchlist ahead of developments around its smart-home strategy.
Iren (IREN): Shares rose 5% in early premarket trade following reports that Anthropic signed a multiyear data center cloud deal with the company.
Amazon (AMZN): Shares inch higher in early premarket trading after reports indicate the company is in talks to acquire AI startup Decart for around $7 billion to enhance its use of custom Trainium chips.
Palantir Technologies (PLTR): Hovering near record highs as retail bulls embrace recent Wall Street upgrades despite its CTO dismissing reports regarding software usage in foreign strikes.
AST SpaceMobile (ASTS): Shares trending higher in early premarket following news that its BlueBird 14–16 satellites arrived at Cape Canaveral for launch, alongside FCC clearance for 900-MHz testing.
Rocket Lab (RKLB): NASA's chief has reportedly warned that the lunar race with China is very 'close', with analysts highlighting expanding commercial opportunities for its Neutron rocket.
Chinese Electric Vehicle Makers (NIO, BYD, XPEV): Watching EU regulatory updates as reports suggest a 350,000-vehicle annual cap on Chinese hybrid imports starting December 1. XPEV is also on the retail radar after executives said its advanced driver-assistance software can adapt to European roads with minimal additional AI training data.
Sellas Life Sciences (SLS): Shares rose in early premarket trade after retail traders speculated that a massive booth reserved for the upcoming ASH conference points toward potential M&A or partnership talks.
Fannie Mae (FNMA), Freddie Mac (FMCC): In his latest Substack note, Michael Burry revealed that he has swapped common shares for preferred stock to manage dilution risks.
SoFi Technologies (SOFI): Shares remained in focus after Keefe, Bruyette & Woods lowered its price target to $15 from $16 and maintained an Underperform rating.
WF Holding (WFF): Pulling back after a massive speculative rally of over 450% on Friday.
Other tickers trending on Stocktwits at the time of writing included Boost Run (BRUN), Rivian (RIVN), Starbucks (SBUX), and Chipotle (CMG).
On the economic front, traders will tune in for Cleveland Fed President Beth Hammack's speech today at 12:10 pm ET, ahead of key inflation data (September CPI on Wednesday and PPI on Thursday) and Fed Chair Kevin Warsh's speech later in the week.

The Morning Bull - US Market Morning Update Monday, Oct, 12 2026
US stocks are set for a cautious positive open, with sentiment hinging on fresh signs of sticky inflation expectations. The single driver this morning is how the Federal Reserve might react to households now seeing higher prices ahead.
The latest University of Michigan survey puts year ahead inflation expectations at 4.7% in October 2026, the highest reading since May. The five year outlook sits at 3.5%. Both figures lean against a quick return to the Fed’s 2% target.
Higher expected inflation can keep Treasury yields elevated, which typically pushes up borrowing costs for households and companies. That pressure can squeeze interest sensitive areas of the market and forces investors to question how generous future profit assumptions should be.
The key tension now is simple: if upcoming US CPI and PPI data fail to confirm this hotter expectations story, investors may need to rethink how aggressively they price in tighter Fed policy over the next few meetings.
Tesla is back in focus after third quarter figures for the Cybertruck pointed to annualized sales of roughly 28,000 to 32,000 units. That is about 13% of Elon Musk’s earlier 250,000 target. Demand trends for the pickup and fresh competition from Ford’s lower priced Fathom EV truck provide a clearer view of Tesla’s electric truck ambitions.
Amazon.com is reported to be in talks to buy Decart AI for about US$7b. Decart builds software that helps developers switch efficiently between different chips. This potential deal aligns with Amazon’s push to make better use of its Trainium processors as AI workloads increase. Watch for updates on whether a rival bidder appears or the negotiations stall.
Citigroup is drawing attention after its rates team flagged a potential shift in how the US Treasury funds itself. The bank expects Treasury to cut US$3b from each 20 year and 30 year bond auction and lean more on short term bills, with a 20 year cancellation even on the table. Any change announced on November 4 could affect long term yields and rate sensitive sectors.
Crown Castle (CCI) jumped 15.60% after SpaceX&#x27;s spectrum deal highlighted potential tower leasing demand and revenue opportunities.
Moderna (MRNA) climbed 14.21% after the NIH cancer vaccine initiative and index inclusion drew attention to its mRNA pipeline.
Humana (HUM) advanced 11.56% as Medicare Advantage star ratings improved and analysts raised earnings expectations with multiple target upgrades.
T-Mobile US (TMUS) fell 13.27% as investors weighed SpaceX&#x27;s spectrum purchase, T-Mobile&#x27;s store closures, and plan changes.
AT&T (T) declined 10.82% following industry concern over SpaceX&#x27;s wireless push alongside AT&T&#x27;s own mobile pricing moves.
AST SpaceMobile (ASTS) fell 10.48% despite analyst comments highlighting increased long term relevance after SpaceX&#x27;s spectrum deal.
US inflation data and a heavy slate of bank earnings sit at the center of the next few sessions, with rate expectations and financials in focus.
US CPI and Core CPI , Wed 14 Oct. Headline inflation is forecast at 3.6% year on year with core at 2.5%. A reading above these marks would pressure rate sensitive growth stocks and could keep Treasury yields and the US dollar firm.
US PPI and Core PPI , Thu 15 Oct. Producer prices are projected to rise 0.5% month on month with core at 0.3%. Stronger prints would signal upstream cost pressure that could squeeze margins for manufacturers and retailers.
US Retail Sales , Thu 15 Oct. Headline sales are expected to rise 0.3% month on month after a 1.2% gain, with ex autos forecast at 0.5%. A softer outcome would challenge the consumer spending story that underpins many cyclical stocks.
JPMorgan Chase , Tue 13 Oct. Q3 results at 6:45 AM ET put loan growth and credit costs in the spotlight for this bellwether bank, shaping how investors think about profitability across the US financial sector.
UnitedHealth Group , Tue 13 Oct. Q3 earnings at 9:30 AM ET make medical cost trends and 2026 guidance key, since any change here can influence sentiment across health insurers and managed care stocks.
Tech has eased over the past week even after a strong year, as hotter inflation expectations keep pressure on interest rates and on richly valued growth stories. News around Apple’s leadership change and Amazon.com’s talks to buy Decart keeps AI in the spotlight but also highlights how much the sector leans on a few mega platforms.
If you hold broad Tech exposure, your returns are closely tied to these giants and to borrowing costs. The next key signal is US CPI on Wednesday, which could reset views on how restrictive Fed policy needs to be for rate sensitive sectors like Tech.
Semiconductors are down over the last seven days after a very strong year to date climb, as traders lock in profits and reassess heavy AI exposure. Stories around Nvidia and Taiwan Semiconductor keep attention on data center and smartphone demand, but higher rate worries make investors more selective.
For chip heavy portfolios, that means more sensitivity to each update on AI infrastructure spending. Taiwan Semiconductor’s Thursday earnings are the key checkpoint because they give a direct read on global chip orders and capacity plans.
Consumer Discretionary : Weak mood, selective spending
Consumer Discretionary has posted a clear 7 day gain even though returns over the past year remain in the red, matching the mixed message from the Michigan sentiment survey. Households say they feel worse, yet activity around Amazon.com’s AI push, travel and dining shows that spending is still happening in pockets.
For you, that can mean a bigger gap between businesses tied to everyday purchases and companies reliant on big ticket, financed items. Thursday’s US Retail Sales report is the main release to watch for this group since it shows whether actual spending is starting to follow sentiment lower.
Energy and the Oil and Gas group both show solid 7 day gains on top of strong year to date returns, helped by still elevated crude prices and insider buying at producers such as Mach Natural Resources. Higher oil supports cash generation for producers, while drivers, airlines and transport heavy firms face higher fuel bills.
If you own Energy exposure, performance is closely tied to the oil price and supply data. The swing factor this week is US crude inventory numbers from API on Wednesday and EIA on Thursday, which will guide views on how tight the market really is.
Financials : Inflation expectations meet bank earnings
Financials have edged higher over the past week after a softer month, with Banks roughly flat on a 7 day view as investors weigh higher rates against credit risk. Headlines around Citigroup’s call on long term Treasury issuance and deals in payments and wealth management show how mixed the revenue picture is.
Everyday investors watch this sector because big banks influence deposit rates, lending standards and card costs. The main catalyst now is the opening batch of US bank earnings from JPMorgan, Bank of America and Citigroup, where comments on loan demand, deposit pricing and consumer credit will show how higher inflation expectations are feeding through to the real economy.
With Treasury yields sensitive to inflation expectations and bank earnings, rate focused investors can start with this list of 31 resilient stocks with low risk scores to see which businesses screen as more resilient if volatility picks up.

### 51. ★★★☆☆ Is the stock market open on Columbus Day? NYSE, Nasdaq hours today - AOL.com

- **分类**: 📈 市场
- **来源**: GN Markets
- **多源确认**: 1

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Labor Day, the unofficial start of fall, has passed, and Halloween is on the horizon. Now it&#x27;s time for the next federal holiday — Columbus Day .
Many people have the day off work, but will the stock market and banks be open on Columbus Day? Here are the details.
This year, Columbus Day will fall on Monday, October 12.
Is the stock market open on Columbus Day 2026? New York Stock Exchange, Nasdaq hours
According to the U.S. Office of Personnel Management , Columbus Day is a federal holiday. However, both the New York Stock Exchange and the Nasdaq Stock Market will be open .
Here are the NYSE holiday hours for the rest of the year. Nasdaq follows the same hours and holiday schedule:
Thanksgiving Day: Closed (Thursday, Nov. 26; closes at 1 p.m. ET Nov. 27)
Christmas Day: Closed (Friday, Dec. 25; closes at 1 p.m. ET Dec. 24)
Why is there a Columbus Day? Why is it Indigenous Peoples&#x27; Day?
Columbus Day commemorates the voyage and landing of Italian explorer Christopher Columbus in the New World on October 12, 1492.
Columbus Day is a federal holiday. Indigenous Peoples&#x27; Day is not, though it is still recognized as a national holiday. This holiday celebrates the histories, cultures, and perspectives of Indigenous peoples and their ancestors who lived in America before European explorers arrived.
This article originally appeared on Cincinnati Enquirer: Is the stock market open on Columbus Day? NYSE, Nasdaq hours today

### 55. ★★☆☆☆ Documents falsified to evade tax on Abramovich's wealth, BBC finds

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: BBC World / BBC Business
- **多源确认**: 2
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T05:00:52+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Backdated paperwork also made the Russian oligarch’s yachts and mansions disappear from accounts, investigation finds.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Figure caption, Watch: The documents that were falsified to allow Roman Abramovich to evade tax
By James Oliver ,Â  Rory Tinman ,Â  BBC Panorama Â  and Â  Simon Lock ,Â  the Bureau of Investigative Journalism
The world&#x27;s biggest accountancy firm helped in falsifying documents to hide Roman Abramovich&#x27;s wealth and evade tax, BBC Panorama has found.
Accounts signed off by Deloitte in Cyprus meant assets such as superyachts, mansions and private jets worth $672m (Â£508m) vanished from Abramovich&#x27;s company accounts.
Deloitte also appears to have advised on a scheme to evade a huge tax bill on the oligarch&#x27;s wealth through the falsification of documents.
Barrister Andrew Mitchell KC, an expert in financial services law, said Panorama&#x27;s evidence showed the scheme was "straightforward" tax evasion and "it couldn&#x27;t be more blatant".
Roman Abramovich&#x27;s lawyers said he denies that he evaded tax or concealed his fortune. They said Panorama provided no evidence he knew about, authorised or directed the falsification of any document and said it was legitimate to rely on advice from professionals.
Deloitte Cyprus said it "acts in compliance with applicable laws".
The findings are being revealed following a joint investigation between BBC Panorama and the Bureau of Investigative Journalism.
For nearly two decades, Roman Abramovich was the most famous Russian oligarch in the UK, welcomed in London where he bought Chelsea Football Club in 2003 and bankrolled the club&#x27;s most successful period. But after Russia&#x27;s full-scale invasion of Ukraine in 2022, he was sanctioned by the UK government as an associate of President Vladimir Putin and forced to sell the club.
Abramovich has been reported to be living in Turkey, a safe haven for him because it did not apply sanctions. In the Channel Island of Jersey, Â£5.4bn of his assets remain frozen and a criminal investigation has been going on for four years.
The oligarch&#x27;s lawyers told the BBC he denies the allegations in the Jersey investigation and says it was "undertaken for political purposes".
Our investigation has uncovered new evidence about the way Abramovich&#x27;s business empire has been run. It also raises serious questions about the integrity of some of his professional advisers.
Image caption, Chateau de la Croe (top left) in the south of France, Fyning Hill in Sussex and two superyachts vanished from company accounts
The evidence is contained in a leak that includes thousands of documents about the oligarch&#x27;s finances and the operations of a network of offshore companies administered from Cyprus - which used to be a tax haven popular with Russians - between 1995 and 2022.
The BBC and media partners including the Guardian, Paper Trail Media, the Organised Crime and Corruption Reporting Project, and the Cyprus Investigative Reporting Network - as well as the Bureau of Investigative Journalism , external - have been reporting on the leaked files since 2023 as part of the International Consortium of Investigative Journalists&#x27; Cyprus Confidential , external investigation.
In 2022, Panorama also reported on how Abramovich had made his fortune from a corrupt deal to buy a Russian state-owned oil company in the 1990s, selling it back to the government a decade later for billions in profit. He has previously said allegations of corruption in the deal are false.
The profits from Abramovich&#x27;s oil deals in Russia were funnelled into a web of offshore companies. They were administered from Cyprus by a corporate services firm called MeritServus, in Limassol, the island&#x27;s second-largest city.
Until 2005, Deloitte - now the biggest of the "big four" firms which dominate the accounting industry - owned MeritServus. After they split, the two companies worked closely together with Abramovich&#x27;s associates to manage his global fortune.
The leaked documents from MeritServus reveal some of their practices.
Panorama - Roman Abramovich: How He Hid His Fortune
He was the Russian oligarch who brought his wealth to the UK. But whatâs the truth about Roman Abramovichâs fortune? Panorama investigates the documents that were falsified to hide his wealth and evade tax.
Watch now on BBC iPlayer or on BBC One at 20:00 BST on Monday 12 October.
Our first example of backdated paperwork comes in 2006, after a new law in Cyprus meant that company accounts had to be made public. The new transparency law threatened to expose the secrets of Abramovich&#x27;s vast wealth.
Deloitte Cyprus had prepared draft accounts for 2003 - but had not yet finalised them - for his Cypriot company Amiscan Investments. The accounts detailed $672m of luxury property.
A mansion in West Sussex, a luxury flat in London and an estate in the south of France were all included - plus helicopters, private jets and a small fleet of boats the oligarch owned at the time, including the $147.5m Pelorus.
Image caption, Deloitte in Cyprus appears to have advised Abramovich&#x27;s company on a scheme to evade tax
To keep them secret, Abramovich&#x27;s associates and Meritservus decided to rewrite history. They drew up a plan to circumvent the transparency law by transferring the assets to Amiscan&#x27;s parent company in the British Virgin Islands (BVI), where they could be kept hidden. And they backdated the change by three years to make it look like the asset transfer had happened in 2003.
Deloitte Cyprus reviewed the backdated documents and said they were "adequate" to allow them to remove the assets from Amiscan&#x27;s accounts.
When the 2003 accounts were signed off by Deloitte in 2006, the homes, planes and yachts disappeared at the stroke of a pen. Only $75,000 of assets remained.
Deloitte signed off the accounts even though the leaked documents show it knew they were completely misleading.
Lord Sikka, professor of accounting at the University of Sheffield, said it was a "violation of a statutory duty" and "pretty scandalous" to misrepresent transaction dates and not to publish true and fair accounts.
Panorama also found evidence that backdated documents had been used in a tax evasion scheme.
This involved another one of Abramovich&#x27;s Cypriot companies, Kravin Investments, which had almost $2bn in the bank in 2003.
It had been earning large amounts in interest, so Roman Abramovich&#x27;s company should have been paying tax on that interest to the Cypriot authorities.
But his advisers had another idea to rewrite history.
An email from MeritServus in August 2005 to Abramovich&#x27;s Russian associates said a plan had been devised after a meeting with Deloitte. "If you agree, it will be necessary to change the financial statements and the accounting records accordingly for 2003 and 2004," the email read.
MeritServus informed the oligarch&#x27;s Russian associates, that Kravin&#x27;s "Provisional Tax Assessments for 2005" would be filed showing the company earning no income from the money.
A month later, an associate in Russia emailed in response, "I agree".
MeritServus then created a series of backdated documents which falsely purported to show that Kravin was only holding and investing the funds on behalf of its parent company in the BVI.
This made it look like Kravin did not actually own the money, so it would not be liable for tax in Cyprus.
Deloitte wrote in a September 2005 email that "certain changes will have to be made to the accounts (bank account balances, interest income, profit and loss account)" because of the backdated changes to Kravin&#x27;s assets.
So Deloitte was apparently advising on the falsification of financial records. The evidence suggests it allowed Abramovich&#x27;s company to evade millions in tax.
Andrew Mitchell KC told us the scheme was "straightforward" tax evasion. "If it happened in England and it was in the millions, His Majesty&#x27;s Revenue and Customs would be thinking about a prosecution," he said.
Cypriot MP Christos Christofides said that if tax had been evaded, then "we have to see how we can get our money back and how, if some people are guilty, they have to pay the price according to our law."
In addition to the documents which Deloitte helped to backdate, Abramovich&#x27;s advisers at MeritServus falsified a third set of records. This involved the oligarch&#x27;s huge business fortune - more than $10bn - held by a company called Electus Investments in Cyprus.
MeritServus backdated documents to make it look like Electus did not own the fortune.
It used another Abramovich company with exactly the same name of Electus Investments, but this time based in the more secretive BVI, where accounts did not have to be made public.
Emails show that in 2006, MeritServus acknowledged that the fortune was owned by Electus Cyprus. But again it proposed to rewrite the past, this time "to transfer the beneficial ownership to Electus Investments in the BVI" and make it look as though the change had happened three years earlier.
MeritServus signed and backdated documents which falsely purported to show the BVI company had owned the $10bn fortune since 20 November 2003. So when the accounts of the Cypriot company with the same name were made public, it appeared to have assets worth just $145,000.
Roman Abramovich&#x27;s billions had been made to disappear.
Andrew Mitchell KC told Panorama: "What you take away from it is there are professionals who are prepared to bend and break the rules of their profession and therefore the law. And it looks that they will stop literally at nothing in terms of creating documents."
Vladimir Milov, Russia&#x27;s deputy minister of energy as Abramovich built his empire and now a critic of the Russian government, said: "For each Russian oligarch or Middle Eastern or African corrupt oligarch, there&#x27;s an army of Western financial and legal services and PR services providers, who is easily taking money for whitewashing their reputation and making them look normal."
Denying any wrongdoing by the oligarch, Roman Abramovich&#x27;s lawyers said that he was "not involved in the day-to-day operation of those companies" and that where arrangements were proposed by professional advisers, "reliance on that advice was entirely proper and legitimate".
Deloitte Cyprus said it could not comment on the specific allegations because the documents were more than 20 years old and no longer accessible.
MeritServus closed following the Cyprus Confidential leak.

<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

New Apple TV 4K, HomeView, and HomePod Mini: 16 Details Just Leaked
Sunday October 11, 2026 12:56 pm PDT by Joe Rossignol
Just two days before Apple's "Welcome home" experience in New York, Bloomberg 's Mark Gurman has leaked several details about the new products set to be unveiled, including an all-new smart home hub, a HomePod mini 2, and a new Apple TV 4K.
Here is an all-in-one summary of the details shared by Gurman:

- **发生了什么**: MEP Raphaël Glucksmann issues call after becoming social democrat candidate for 2027 presidential electionThe European lawmaker Raphaël Glucksmann has urged the French centre left to wage an “existential battle” to prevent Marine Le Pen’s far right winning the 2027 presidential election after he was chosen as the social democrat candidate for the presidency.“We’ve got to rid our hearts of the spirit of defeat,” Glucks

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Raphaël Glucksmann said the 2027 presidential election campaign would be brutal and ‘like no other we have ever experienced’. Photograph: Mohammed Badra/EPA
Raphaël Glucksmann said the 2027 presidential election campaign would be brutal and ‘like no other we have ever experienced’. Photograph: Mohammed Badra/EPA
French centre left must wage ‘existential battle’ to prevent far right winning presidency
MEP Raphaël Glucksmann issues call after becoming social democrat candidate for 2027 presidential election
The European lawmaker Raphaël Glucksmann has urged the French centre left to wage an “existential battle” to prevent Marine Le Pen ’s far right winning the 2027 presidential election after he was chosen as the social democrat candidate for the presidency.
“We’ve got to rid our hearts of the spirit of defeat,” Glucksmann, the founder of the small party Place Publique, said on Monday morning after his decisive win in a centre-left primary race held by the Socialist party.
In his first interview since being designated a presidential candidate on Sunday night, Glucksmann told France Inter radio that the country was living in a “historic moment”, with the far right at the gates of power. He said the 2027 presidential election campaign would be brutal and “like no other we have ever experienced”. He said it would be unbearable to have a centre left “that doesn’t believe in victory”, adding that he could restore a “pride in social democracy”.
At present the centre left sits substantially behind the far right in the polls and also trails the radical left politician Jean-Luc Mélenchon , who is polling high but with whom Glucksmann refuses to work.
Glucksmann, 46, an essayist and intellectual who argues he is very different to career politicians, is staunchly pro-Europe and pro-Ukraine. His candidacy marks the first time the Socialist party will not have its own presidential candidate since the party was founded in 1969. The party suffered two crushing defeats in the last two presidential elections.
Glucksmann has been a member of the European parliament since 2019. He strikes a more reformist and centrist tone than the Socialist party, but has cooperated with it in the past, presenting joint electoral lists. He has made no secret of his presidential ambitions but needed the Socialist party machine behind him for his campaign.
Glucksman is polling at between 9% and 14%, well behind the frontrunner, Le Pen. He also trails the centre-right contender Édouard Philippe, who served as prime minister under Emmanuel Macron.
Crucially, he is also behind Mélenchon, whose party, La France Insoumise, boycotted the Socialist primary and has presented itself as the biggest force in a divided left.
Glucksmann is tasked with regaining centre-left voters who jumped ship to support Macron, though his critics on the left accuse him of being too centrist. He said the French president, who is standing down next spring, “wasted 10 years” failing to act on the climate emergency.
Glucksmann is known for being a campaigner on international issues. He took part in documentary work to raise awareness of the aftermath of genocide in Rwanda and is a former adviser to the anti-Moscow former Georgian president Mikheil Saakashvili.
Glucksmann, who has been accused by opponents of being too much of a Paris intellectual, said he “lost sleep at night” worrying about how the social-democratic left had lost the working class in France. He said the centre left needed to regain “a form of radicality that we lost” and the focus of this would be environmental policy.
He told the daily Libération he made no apology for being an intellectual and would use his background to “better explain the moment we’re living in” rather than relying on “slogans”.
Glucksmann’s partner, Léa Salamé, is the star presenter of the French public broadcaster’s flagship evening news programme. She has stepped down from presenting the news while he runs for president.

Natural Gas News: Weekly Forecast Sees Warm Weather Capping Gas Rally
Sellers are likely to retain the edge while warm weather holds national demand in shoulder-season range.
Returning Gulf output and projected end-October storage near 3,985 Bcf should limit follow-through buying.
Holding above $3.146 could keep the range intact; a failure through $3.087 would expose the August-September bottoms.
Storm Short-Covering Fades as Warm Weather Caps the Rally
November natural gas futures are back under pressure after Hurricane Isaias gave shorts a reason to cover going into the weekend. Gulf output is already coming back online. The weather models are not helping. The market is sitting right on top of the retracement zone that has been defining the range. The minor trend is down and the storm did not change that.
At 06:55 GMT, November natural gas futures were trading at $3.171, down $0.033 or 1.03%.
Next up Silver (XAG) Forecast: Counter-Trend Buyers Looking to Extend Rally Over $60.835
Weekly November Natural Gas Futures Technical Analysis
November natural gas futures  are trading lower at the start of the week. The main trend is up according to the weekly swing chart, but the minor trend is down. This is helping to create volatility and two-sided trading.
A trade through $3.395 will signal a resumption of the main uptrend. A move through $2.896 will change the main trend to down.
The minor trend will change to up on a move through $3.395. A trade through $2.912 will signal a resumption of the minor downtrend.
The key area to watch this week is the retracement zone at $3.146 to $3.087. Last week, the market straddled this area before moving higher. Early Monday, November natural gas is trading on the strong side of this zone.
A sustained move over $3.146 will signal the presence of buyers. If this creates enough upside momentum, we could see a test of another retracement zone at $3.264 to $3.350. The latter is a potential trigger point for an acceleration to the upside with a 50% level at $3.588, the 52-week moving average at $3.618 and a 50% level at $3.769 possible targets.
A sustained move under $3.087 will indicate the presence of sellers. This could create the downside momentum to challenge the three bottoms at $2.912, $2.902 and $2.896.
Trader reaction to $3.146 to $3.087 is likely to set the tone this week. The mixed trends on the main and minor swing charts, however, are not providing a clear direction. The position of the retracement zone at $3.264 to $3.350 indicates any rally will be labored and possibly limited by the 52-week moving average. Sellers could also face challenges at the three bottoms.
Every new Natural Gas analysis as it publishes, today&#x27;s technical signal and key levels, live price — on one page.
Gulf producers shut in 59% of output Friday and Lower-48 production dropped to 109.3 Bcf per day, the lowest since January. November natural gas settled at $3.220, up $0.052 or 1.64%. That was shorts stepping aside after Thursday’s storage report knocked the market off its highs. The restart timeline is what matters now. Production was already coming back over the weekend.
The weather models are not giving buyers anything to work with. High pressure has kept most of the country in the 60s, 70s and 80s with 90s and 100-degree readings still possible in California and the Southwest. The Great Lakes and Northeast have seen some early heating demand but that is regional. NatGasWeather sees low demand near term and only moderate demand later in the forecast. Commodity Weather Group has called for above-average temperatures through mid-October. The Lower 48 is stuck in shoulder season and the storm did not change that.
Last week’s injection came in lighter than the five-year average. That made shorts more careful heading into the storm. It did not change where inventories sit. Storage is still above the seasonal norm, and the EIA sees it finishing October at the highest level in a decade. Then the agency raised its production forecast for next year. That is not a number buyers can work with.
Baker Hughes had rigs down one last week. Nobody flinched. LNG exports were firm Friday. Good number. Not good enough. Exports are absorbing gas. They are not absorbing enough of it.
Gulf production is coming back online after Isaias and the warm forecast through mid-October keeps national demand in shoulder-season range. Storage above the five-year average with end-of-October projections at 3,985 Bcf gives sellers the bigger number. The short-covering rally did not produce a new buyer. It produced a pause.
November natural gas is trading on the strong side of the retracement zone early Monday, and the reaction at that level sets the tone for the week. The mixed trends on the main and minor swing charts mean the range could hold in either direction. The resistance above is labored with the 52-week moving average waiting at $3.618. The three bottoms below are still down there if sellers take control back. The storm gave the market a breath. The supply picture took it right back.

### 59. ★★☆☆☆ Weekly Radar 005: AI Demand Pulls DRAM Supply Toward Servers - TrendForce

- **分类**: 🌍 地缘政治
- **来源**: GN AI
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T10:04:48+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Weekly Radar 005: AI Demand Pulls DRAM Supply Toward Servers&nbsp;&nbsp;TrendForce

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Weekly Radar 005: AI Demand Pulls DRAM Supply Toward Servers
Meanwhile, smartphone and notebook ODMs slow consumer MLCC inventory builds.
Memory and storage supply remain tight well into 2027. DRAM stays Very Tight at 20 weeks versus an 8-week balanced level, while suppliers are shifting bit supply from PC DRAM toward server DRAM. CSPs are also moving to secure eSSD and HDD supply through 2027, with HDD lead times still at 50 weeks.
Terafab puts foundry strategy back in focus ahead of TSMC earnings. Musk says Tesla and SpaceX plan to build and operate the fab themselves, with Intel still involved and any TSMC role potentially limited to a sublease. TSMC reports this week, putting capex, margins and the next phase of AI-driven capacity expansion back under the spotlight
The OCP Global Summit opens this week, where the AI supply chain tackles its next set of bottlenecks. As racks move toward megawatt scale, watch for progress on 800 VDC power, liquid cooling, and optical networking, including co-packaged and near-packaged optics.
TrendForce Substack is reader-supported. To receive new posts and support our work, consider becoming a free or paid subscriber.
We track CPU, GPU, DRAM, NAND, HDD, ABF, and MLCC each week. Start with the status color, then compare the current lead time with the balanced-market benchmark and read the quick take.
Samsung Guides for Record 3Q26 Operating Profit amid Memory Boom
What Happened: Samsung’s preliminary 3Q26 guidance points to a blockbuster quarter. Operating profit exploded 782.5% year over year to KRW 107.4 trillion, smashing through the KRW 100 trillion mark for the first time, while sales more than doubled to KRW 195 trillion, up 126.6%.
Why It Matters: The guidance adds to evidence that the memory upcycle is far from over, following Micron’s strong earnings in September. Conventional DRAM prices continue to climb as supply remains tight, and HBM4 is now a meaningful contributor, riding demand for NVIDIA’s Vera Rubin. However, the picture could be less rosy outside memory chips: soaring memory costs are squeezing Samsung’s smartphone and appliance business, and the market will watch closely when the full earnings release on October 29 breaks down results by division, with the DX unit’s losses expected to widen.
What Happened: Intel isn’t walking away from Musk’s Terafab. Responding to market chatter that TSMC could be tapped for the project, Intel CEO Lip-Bu Tan said the chipmaker will stay involved. Musk then moved to cap the TSMC speculation, saying the foundry giant could sublease part of Terafab “if they want, but nothing more than that.” At the October 10 X Takeover event, Musk called chipmaking “one of the hardest things to do in the world” and said mastering it could take his companies “quite a few years.” Even so, he set a goal of advancing around 10 chip designs a year, far above the industry’s roughly two years per major design.
Why It Matters: Terafab could remain a key external customer win for Intel’s foundry ambitions. Musk’s comments show SpaceX still plans to build and run the fab itself, leaving little room for TSMC beyond a possible sublease, especially as the foundry already has multiple fabs to build in Arizona. Analysts add that with the U.S. government now an Intel shareholder, it would be hard to see the chipmaker displaced to any significant extent.
AMD’s Lisa Su Heads to Korea to Secure Memory Supply, Eyeing 3-5 Year Partnerships with Samsung, SK hynix
What Happened: AMD CEO Lisa Su made a trip to South Korea to meet memory heavyweights Samsung and SK hynix, confirming that HBM4 shipments for the MI455 are getting underway. She also floated supply-chain partnerships spanning three to five years or more, a sign that the AI chip giant wants to lock in memory supply as shortages persist.
Why It Matters: Long-term agreements are fast becoming big tech’s playbook for securing scarce memory, and AMD seems to be following it. However, the reported deal could matter even more for Samsung: with AMD still heavily reliant on TSMC for its CPUs and GPUs, deeper memory ties could give Samsung leverage in foundry talks, as market speculation grows that AMD may adopt Samsung’s 2nm node.
Event: The 2026 OCP (Open Compute Project) Global Summit opens Oct 12 in San Jose and runs through Oct 15, expanded to four days under the theme “Scaling Innovation for the AI Era.”
Why It Matters: From hyperscalers like Meta and Google to chip heavyweights NVIDIA, AMD, Broadcom and Marvell and server rack makers, the AI supply chain gathers at OCP to tackle the bottlenecks of large-scale AI: power, cooling, networking and memory. A key focus is 800 VDC, a high-voltage power architecture NVIDIA, Google and Microsoft are working to establish as an open industry standard.
Key Watch Points: 800 VDC Power Architecture, Liquid Cooling, AI Networking & Optics:
As AI racks head toward megawatt scale, watch for progress on an open 800 VDC standard and vendor hardware, wider adoption of liquid cooling over air cooling, and optical interconnects, including co-packaged and near-packaged optics, to extend AI networks beyond copper.
Event: ASML earnings: the chip industry’s bellwether steps up
Why It Matters: ASML is the sole maker of EUV lithography machines, the tools Intel, TSMC and Samsung rely on for leading-edge chips. Its results offer a real-time read on the semiconductor cycle and how aggressively foundries and memory makers are pushing advanced nodes.
After lifting its 2026 forecast twice, to €43–45 billion in sales and a 54–56% gross margin, will ASML raise it again? Investors will also parse its 2027 outlook for signs foundry and memory spending will last, and watch whether China, already down to 14% of 2Q26 sales, slips further amid U.S. restrictions.
Event: TSMC to report 3Q26 earnings, guided for record revenue of $44.6–45.8B while 2nm ramp and overseas expansion could weigh on margins
Why It Matters: With NVIDIA and AMD among its biggest customers, TSMC’s guidance is the clearest gauge of how long the AI chip boom can run. After adding $100B to its Arizona plan in July, investors will watch how tariffs and geopolitics, and rivals’ advances, shape its next moves.
Key Watch Points: Capex and Margins, Advanced Node/ CoWoS Roadmap, Possible Terafab Tie-up:
With another record quarter already guided, focus shifts to how higher capex (2026 budget of $60–64B, with 2027 plans in focus) and costly ramps will affect margins. Management has flagged 3–4 points of dilution from the 2nm ramp in 2H26, plus 2–3 points from overseas fabs at early stages. Rivalry with Intel continues on both foundry (A16/A14 vs. Intel’s 14A) and advanced packaging (CoWoS vs. EMIB), while a possible tie-up with Musk’s Terafab, which Intel has joined, adds another wrinkle.

</details>

### 60. ★★☆☆☆ Stellantis CEO urges incentives for carmakers who build in Europe as Chinese rivals gain ground

- **分类**: 🇪🇺 欧洲
- **来源**: CNBC Top
- **多源确认**: 1
- **时间**: 2026-10-12T08:41:50+00:00
- **发生了什么**: Stellantis' CEO urged European regulators to create incentives for carmakers to manufacture vehicles in the region amid competition from Chinese rivals.

<details>
<summary><strong>原文 / 抓取到的正文</strong></summary>

Stellantis CEO urges rewards for Europe carmakers amid Chinese competition
Stellantis CEO told CNBC that Europe needs to incentivize carmakers who build in the region.
It comes as Chinese carmakers are quickly accelerating their share of the European car market.
Shares of Stellantis, known for its key car brands such as Jeep and Ram, rose in early trade Monday after the EU and China reached an agreement to cut the number of hybrid cars China exports to the bloc.
Stellantis CEO backs level playing field for all carmakers
Stellantis&#x27; CEO has called on European regulators to create stronger incentives for carmakers to manufacture vehicles in the region, as it faces intensifying competition from Chinese rivals that are dominating the market.
Antonio Filosa, who has served at the helm of the automotive giant since 2025, spoke with "CNBC Conversation" ahead of the Paris Motor Show on Monday about the company&#x27;s declining sales amid China&#x27;s "impressive" acceleration in the European market.
"What we are supporting a lot in Europe is to establish for everybody a level playing field," Filosa said in an interview with CNBC&#x27;s Charlotte Reed .
"In concrete terms, it means to work on true regulation... starting from "Made in Europe," that should be regulation that incentivizes, rewards, and protects the ones that are able to demonstrate that they design cars, engineer cars," Filosa added.
Made in Europe refers to proposed legislation drawn up by the EU Commission which would require carmakers to meet minimum local content requirements to sell within the regional market.
The global carmaker, which is known for its key car brands such as Jeep and Ram, has been in the midst of a $70 billion turnaround strategy as it struggles with declining demand. The company&#x27;s shares are down almost 60% so far since the beginning of the year.
Stellantis&#x27; shares since the beginning of the year.
Chinese car brands are dominating the global market , with their global auto-sales share jumping by nearly 70% from 2020 to 2025, according to market research and consulting firm GlobalData. Meanwhile, the market share of European car sales was close to zero in 2020, but is up to 12% in August this year, according to data from Germany-based Dataforce.
"I believe that Chinese automakers, the ones that we know, can really offer many consumers in many segments, good technology, and competitive prices," Filosa said.
European trade commissioner Maroš Sefčovič said Friday that China has agreed to halve its exports of hybrid cars to the bloc, after Beijing had repeatedly rejected requests.
European carmakers moved higher Monday morning after news of the agreement. Stellantis added 2.7% and French carmaker Renault were up around 3%. French automotive parts suppliers Valeo and Forvia also rose in early trading.
A spokesperson for China&#x27;s Ministry of Commerce responded on Monday to the request, saying that China and the European Union have maintained a dialogue on trade and investment and reached "several consensuses and achievements that are conducive to maintaining a stable and balanced key trade partnership," according to CNBC&#x27;s translation from Chinese to English.
As part of the agreement, one principle laid out was "non-discrimination," which says that no measures will specifically target or discriminate against Chinese companies. "China&#x27;s quota will not be lower than that of any other import source for the EU."
— CNBC&#x27;s Michael Wayland helped contribute to this report.
Choose CNBC as your preferred source on Google and never miss a moment from the most trusted name in business news.

</details>
